# MEH-1249 — risk tiers for docs/MANUAL_TESTING.md

> **GENERATED — do not hand-edit.** Re-run `python3 scripts/qa/tier-manual-testing.py`.
> Source: `docs/MANUAL_TESTING.md` · as-of **2026-09-01** · **1654** checklist items.

Sapir's ruling (01/09) is **risk-based, not coverage-based**: the 580 items the
1,074-row matrix never classified do not get filled in. Every item is tagged, and
only tiers 1 and 2 are verified before launch.

**Tier 3 is not blank — it is accepted.** An unverified item that *looks* verified
is the actual risk; a documented acceptance is not. That is the whole point of
this file.

| Tier | Meaning | Pre-launch | Count |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | critical user journey | **verify** | **581** |
| **2** | LOCK — licensed businesses · manual approval · no transaction fees | **verify** | **105** |
| **3** | everything else | `not verified — accepted, 2026-09-01` | **968** |
| | | **total** | **1654** |

**Tier 1 + 2 = 686 items — that number is what pre-launch QA costs.**

## How an item was tagged

Keyword match over **the item's own text plus its section heading** — a terse line
inside a licensing section inherits that context. **LOCK is tested first**: it is
narrower and higher-stakes, so an item touching a lock is tier 2 even when it also
mentions a journey word. The tier-1 vocabulary is taken from the journeys this repo
already treats as canonical (`e2e/flows/01`–`05`, `18`), not invented here.

The classifier ships with `--self-test`, and `main()` refuses to emit this file if
it fails. Four of its cases are lifted from the real document.
